ParlaMint-ES_2020-05-20-CD200520.u2	Thank you very much. Discussion of the request for authorization of the extension of the state of alarm declared by Royal Decree 463/2020 of 14 March, declaring the state of alarm for the management of the situation of health crisis caused by COVID-19, extended by Royal Decree 476/2020 of 27 March, 487/2020 of 10 April, 492/2020 of 24 April and 514/2020 of 8 May. I should like to inform you that the proposals concerning the request for authorisation of the extension of the state of alarm will be voted upon at the end of the debate on the first item on the agenda. In order to explain the reasons for the request for an extension, the President of the Government has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-05-20-CD200520.u3	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, good morning. Ten weeks have passed since we declared the state of alarm last March 14th and adopted very severe measures of social isolation; a time that we have spent together since this pandemic took the lives of family members, friends, loved ones, interrupted the daily life of our country - also of the world - truncated our way of living, compromised the viability of business, jobs and projects. What seemed to belong to another time ended up happening and what no one expected came. These ten weeks have left a trail of pain that will last forever in the memory of our country. Today we are not all, today we are missing more than 27,700 compatriots, to whom we have just devoted a minimum tribute of silence and to whom we will pay a great tribute, as soon as health conditions permit. Ladies and gentlemen, it has been ten weeks and, therefore, let me make a brief chronological review of the way we have gone so far. On 10 March, I participated in the extraordinary meeting of the European Council where the crisis of COVID-19 was first addressed. And then I advanced that the European Commission and also the Eurogroup would announce joint economic and financial measures. I went on to say that we were faced with a global health emergency, which was affecting dozens of countries and therefore a coordinated global response was needed at European and multilateral level. We work in a coordinated and priority way with the autonomous communities, with social actors and also with political forces. That day, I said: To combat this public health emergency, we will do what is necessary, where it is needed and when it is needed. And I advanced that together - no doubt - we will overcome this crisis. Today, ladies and gentlemen, I hold all those words one by one. I then announced that we were working from the Government on a shock plan, on a triple network that are ultimately four pillars in different areas. The first is to protect and guarantee the well-being of families, especially working families; secondly, to ensure the supply and production of medical supplies and access to health supplies; thirdly, to protect employment in affected sectors as a result of the closure of the health emergency and, fourthly, to put all our efforts and all the resources necessary to support the liquidity of autonomous, small and medium-sized enterprises, ultimately the productive sector of our country. On 11 March, the World Health Organization formally declared that the outbreak of COVID-19 was a pandemic, because the disease spread worldwide and we lacked - as we now lack - the medical treatment that would allow us to protect ourselves from the virus. On the same day, I will reiterate that this pandemic has a triple consequence, which is not only health, but also economic and social. Therefore, we were faced - as we faced - with an unprecedented emergency that lacked a true instruction manual. Only knowledge and science could guide us - although with certain limitations because, obviously, they are also facing an unknown virus from science - in order to be able to make decisions together and in a coordinated way. Spain was overflowing with disease and pain at full speed, and society began to visualize with amazement the nightmare that we were already living. And in the face of an exceptional situation, ladies and gentlemen, there was only one exceptional response from this House as we did. We began to adopt and implement immediately different packages of health, social and economic measures so as not to leave anyone behind; doing what is needed - as I said before - where it is needed and when it is needed. On 13 March, forty-eight hours after the World Health Organization declared COVID a pandemic, I announced the statement of the state of alarm by the Government, which was formally decreed the following day, on 14 March. We then started an accelerated mitigation phase with a clear goal: to contain the virus, avoid contagion and save lives. And for that, what we had to do was bend the curve, and do it in as little time as possible and with as little loss as possible. We all as a community had a mission, a common goal, the most important and the most effective - I repeat - stay at home, take care of those who take care of us, those who take care of us. Ladies and gentlemen, we were the first European country to declare the state of alarm both in terms of the number of contagions and also in terms of deaths on that date. And I would like to stress once again that Spain has always, always, always followed the recommendations of the World Health Organization, of the international health experts - also European - and, no doubt, of the extraordinary work of the Centre for the Coordination of Alerts and Health Emergencies, to whom we will always thank their professionalism, their dedication and their commitment. Let me remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that on Wednesday, February 12, 2020, the organizers of the Mobile World Congress in Barcelona decided to cancel the event due to the resignation of some companies and manufacturers to attend this event. Its reason, as is well known, was the fear that the presence of some companies on the Asian continent, especially China and Korea, in the face of the possibility of a contagion of the disease, which at that moment we saw from Europe circumscribed to the Asian continent. Good proof of this is the reaction that the cancellation provoked in the public opinion of our country. Just read the editorials of the most relevant media at the time to remember what COVID-19 looked like from Spain. There was talk of precipitation, of unwarranted alarm, of an epidemic of fear; in short, they were recurrent expressions not only by the opposition, which criticized the Government for the cancellation of the Mobile World Congress, but also by the most significant media in our country. And they did it without exception, there were no opposing opinions. The COVID-19 was still in mid-February, ladies and gentlemen, a diffuse, remote threat seemingly far from us; the bias of retrospect had not even entered the scene, and so it was during the following weeks. However, just thirty days later, our country was forced to declare the state of alarm at the presence of explosive outbreaks of the pandemic in our territory, one of the first affected in the world by the spread of COVID-19. In just thirty days we passed from widespread disbelief to the need to plunge the entire country into an unprecedented confinement in our history, as the only alternative to avoid the collapse of our National Health System and the death of hundreds of thousands of compatriots. Ladies and gentlemen, exactly the same process has been experienced by each and every one of the great Western countries, which, one after another, have had to adopt - some after certain hesitations, let us not forget - measures of general containment of the population; Italy, France, Germany, Great Britain, then the United States, and now we are seeing it extended to the whole of the American continent. Each and every one of the great Western powers has had to take the same measures and have experienced the same tragedy as our country. The pandemic, therefore, ladies and gentlemen, has not been a problem for our country. The situation allowed us to see that this pandemic does not understand borders, we have said this on many occasions from this rostrum; it does not understand borders, it does not understand ways of thinking, neither of colors nor of ideology nor of gender. The virus affects people without asking where they are born, where they live or what they think, but its consequences, ladies and gentlemen, do have an unequal influence on the population depending on the situation in which each person is found. On 17 March, four days after announcing the state of alarm decree and thus paralysing much of our economy, I announced in this rostrum the mobilization of up to 200 billion euros, the largest amount of economic resources in our country's democratic history, to curb the recession that came as a result of the closure of economic activity. This is the largest social and business protection network in our history, which undoubtedly responds to the magnitude of the economic and social challenge ahead. This crisis leaves us, in my opinion, great lessons to the Government, but the most visible is that the capacity of our public system is the best containment dam against any threat and, therefore, we must defend it, strengthen it and preserve it during the pandemic and, without a doubt, after the pandemic, and translate the applause that is spreading every day in our country, as a result of this support given to health personnel, into lasting public policies that protect us all, I insist, think what you think, live where you live, vote what you vote for, everyone, and this, ladies and gentlemen, is what the Government of Spain is doing. On 18 March, I announced in this House that the Government would promote a study and evaluation committee that will rigorously analyse the state of public health in our country and will therefore draw up a white paper in order to make the necessary adjustments and changes, and that we would also present budgets for social and economic reconstruction as soon as possible, with the support of parliamentary groups, when we had overcome the virus and therefore assessed the economic and social impact of this emergency. On 4 April, I urged the political forces, also the autonomous communities, the municipal governments, the political forces, in short, and the social actors to start working on a pact for social and economic reconstruction on the basis of unity and loyalty. And we agreed, days later, that this pact should be established within the framework of a parliamentary committee, which has already been set up and where the working groups have already been approved by thematic areas. We live, therefore, ladies and gentlemen, very hard weeks, tragic weeks in which everything seemed black: the sanitary workers fought unequally against a lethal enemy, the victims numbered hundreds, and the solution did not settle. A total of two weeks was decreed - let us remember, ladies and gentlemen - of all non-essential activities taking advantage of the celebration of Holy Week. And I want to stress that the response of the population has been and is formidable: older, younger, students, teachers, women and men workers, entrepreneurs, all our fellow countrymen united fulfilled their mission with social discipline and also with morale of victory. And the progress has come. With the effort of all, with the sacrifice of all, these advances have come. Little by little we were recovering the pulse, first, with the return to work after Holy Week; from 27 April, with gradual measures of relief from confinement starting, for example, for children under the age of fourteen, boys and girls; later, on 2 May, we increased the relief measures for the rest of the people. In short, ladies and gentlemen, we were going with a lot of effort to defeat the pandemic. In the meantime, and since the beginning of April, we have been weeks, as I have also announced from this rostrum, working on a plan of descalation, which we have called a plan of transition to the new normality, and for this we seek the opinion and proposals of experts in the scientific, health, social, economic and business fields. In parallel and also coordinated, meetings were held and proposals were exchanged with the heads of the regional and local administrations and, no doubt, also with the social partners. And on April 28, I presented this plan, the result of a collaborative, shared, responsible work, after approving it in the Council of Ministers. I want to be in this, ladies and gentlemen, quite clear. It has not brought us the current, we have not come this far out of inertia; we have come this far with the extreme work of the health workers and the sacrifice, commitment and social discipline of all the citizens of our country. It is the Spanish people who have brought the virus back. It is the citizens of this country who have doubled the curve, united. It is the Spaniards who have stopped the virus together. And no one, I repeat, has the right to waste what we have achieved among all of us during these long weeks of confinement. As you know, 70% of our territory is currently in phase 1 of the descalation. As a result, Spain is reactivating, citizens are regaining freedom of movement, the economic fabric is gradually starting up, and it is true that, in the opinion of health experts, Madrid, Barcelona, its metropolitan area, and some health areas of Castilla and León must remain at the moment in phase 0, although they do so with measures that significantly soften the rigour of restrictions in the commercial field and also in certain recreational activities. And the Canary Islands and Balearic Islands, La Graciosa, El Hierro, La Gomera and Formentera are already in phase 2. As I warned, the plan for the transition to this new normality, the plan of descalation, is asymmetrical, gradual and coordinated. However, asymmetry does not mean disunity or disorder. We'll go out at different speeds, but we'll go out together. There's no way out of the mess or the division. Ladies and gentlemen, in my appearances I have always maintained that the first achievement would come when we overcame as a population, as a community, the peak of the spread of the disease and doubled the curve, and I have to admit to you that we have achieved it, that we have recognized it and that the Spanish have done it. That the second achievement would come when the jump of medical discharges was greater than that of new infected, and I simply leave you this data: this last week, that is, from Monday 11 to Sunday 17, there have been 15,785 cured and cured compared to the 3947 new diagnosed; the previous week were 8316 new infected. It has been so for days and in a sustained way: the curates multiply while the number of new diagnoses is reduced. We have therefore far surpassed the second milestone referred to earlier. The third achievement, which we have not yet achieved but which we are on the way to achieving, would take place when the contagions fall into bites. It is true that we have made progress and a lot - here are the figures - with a lot of effort, with a lot of sacrifice and also with the spirit of victory of the Spanish society as a whole. Let us recall some figures, ladies and gentlemen, two months ago the virus spread at a rate of 35% daily, each person infected transmitted the disease to more than three people on average. Today, the reproduction rate is below, much below, one, around 0.20. But we must assume that, until we have that therapeutic remedy or the vaccine, we will live with the virus because it will not have disappeared from our life and society. And the threat is especially acute during this second stage, in which we have entered that descalation, because there are still hundreds of daily infections that we must detect, attend to and isolate. Therefore, what I want to say to you, ladies and gentlemen, is that prudence and caution must be a standard of conduct in the political decisions that we take among all. And with all that prudence and with all that caution, without lowering the guard, we can say that the state of alarm has worked, the state of alarm, ladies and gentlemen, has worked, and that the plan of descalation is working; it has benefited the whole of autonomous communities and, consequently, the whole of citizens live where they live. We also have, after almost ten weeks since the state of alarm, some certainties that I would like to share with all of you. Last week, for example, we communicated the first wave or the first phase of the study of seroprevalence, unpublished in Europe today, which has coordinated the Ministry of Health and the Ministry of Science together with the leadership of the Carlos III Public Institute, and which has been carried out - and I would like to acknowledge this - with the invaluable collaboration of the autonomous communities, without which it would not have been possible. This study reveals three issues that I would like to share with all of you; three data of enormous value. The first is that the infection has unequally affected the different territories. There are provinces where the percentage of people who have been in contact with the virus is ten times higher than in other provinces, even in neighbouring provinces. The second fact is that only 5%, only 5% of citizens have been infected with COVID-19, with or without symptoms. And the third fact is that the actual lethality of COVID-19 is around 1%, being much higher among older people and with previous illnesses, as we have unfortunately seen during these weeks. From this study I would like to keep two lessons to share with all of you. The first lesson is that the state of alarm has benefited the whole country, all citizens, all autonomous communities alike. The most infected areas have benefited, for example, the Community of Madrid, where the contagions have clearly fallen; and the less affected areas have benefited because the infections have not arrived from outside: Murcia, Andalusia, the Canary Islands, the Balearic Islands and so many others. I insist, the state of alarm has been effective for all the territories and, consequently, has benefited all Spanish citizens. The second lesson is that the path we are on together - and I would also like to underline this and thank this House, the parliamentary majority in this House that has supported the extension of the state of alarm over these long weeks - is the only possible way to combat the virus effectively. Prudence, caution and safety as a guide have been vital to curbing the spread of the virus. Look, at the beginning of the emergency, there were those - not only in Spain, but also outside our country, even important leaders from important countries - who said that the only possible solution would be to allow the virus to circulate freely until it reached what epidemiological experts call herd immunity; that is, 60% or 70% of the population would become infected and, therefore, immunized, and so the virus would cease to circulate. Today we know that, if we had followed that strategy, two thirds of the Spanish population would have been infected, that is, thirty million compatriots and, consequently, today we would have added more than 300,000 victims. Probably, even, much more because the National Health System would obviously have collapsed and, as a result, there would have been far more deaths than we have, unfortunately, recorded today as well. These ladies and gentlemen, they are not conjectures, they are not assumptions; they are facts, they are certain conclusions. As I said before, thanks to all the Members who, over these long weeks, have supported the state of alarm, the extension of the state of alarm, because with their vote they have prevented the spread of the virus and saved thousands and thousands and thousands of lives of compatriots. Thank you. We have recovered a lot of ground, ladies and gentlemen, in the face of the virus, a lot of ground, but the virus is still there, it is still lurking, as we have said on many occasions, and to overcome it it is essential to continue to anticipate us and keep it on track in order to avoid rebounds and possible future waves. And we can only anticipate our enemy if we bet on security at every stage of the descalation, if we bet on coordination, collaboration, solidarity and institutional loyalty. Because descalada is not an exam, nor is it a competition between autonomous communities; descalada is an exercise of science and also of prudence. Ladies and gentlemen, I came under the control of this House on 25 March, on 9 and 22 April, on 6 May, where I had the opportunity to request extensions of the state of alarm from your ladies and gentlemen under the advice of the experts and for the essential purpose of continuing to save lives, to avoid the collapse of our National Health System and to continue to support the workers and the productive fabric of our country. However, this extension has logically different characteristics because we are at a completely different stage from last 14 March. The Government's proposal, which I submit to you for consideration and vote, is that in this extension the only delegated authority of the Government is Minister Illa, the Ministry of Health. The Minister will continue to logically count on the advice of the department's technicians, will maintain the connection and full coordination with the autonomous communities. He will be the person, together with the autonomous communities, who marks the rhythms and modalities of the descalation, and the exceptional powers that were conferred at the beginning of the state of alarm to the Ministry of Defence, the Ministry of the Interior and the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda, will fall. By the way - I have them here - I would like to thank the Ministry of Defence, the Ministry of the Interior and the Ministry of Transport for the work they have done over these ten weeks of state of alarm. [[Applauds]] In addition, in this period, as it has begun to happen for two weeks now, it will govern a basic principle for this government, which is that of co-governance. It is the autonomous governments, the autonomous communities, that have always maintained their full management capacity in their health skills, and this I would also like to underline, remind, reiterate, if I may. The Royal Decree declaring the state of alarm on 14 March effectively established a centralized command, which would have to coordinate all matters relating to the health emergency, but did not relieve the councillors of the autonomous communities from any of their functions - from none, I repeat, of any -. I was literally saying the following, and I would like to recall it for the record in the Journal of Sessions: Each administration - said that decree - will retain the powers conferred on it by the legislation in force in the ordinary management of its services to take the measures it deems necessary under the direct orders of the competent authority for the purposes of the state of alarm. And later the drafting of this decree continued: The autonomous public administrations - also the local ones - will maintain the management, within their area of competence, of the corresponding health services, ensuring at all times their proper functioning. Ladies and gentlemen, it is good that we stress this so as not to confuse the citizenry. Ten weeks later, in my opinion, everything is much clearer. During the state of alarm, the Government has established the legal framework through decrees, ministerial orders, but at no time has it replaced the ordinary authorities in the management of the services. I say this in another way: the autonomous communities have never lost their co-responsibility in the exercise of competences, they have only been coordinated and strengthened in terms of public health, public order and mobility. Well, in addition to continuing to exercise all its managerial powers with full normality - I am referring to the autonomous communities - in addition, in this last stage of the descalation they will recover their full decision-making capacity. Thus, the autonomous communities, and especially their primary care services, will be gaining more and more prominence in the management of the coming weeks of descalation. Consequently, I wish the Autonomous Communities every success and assure them of all loyal support from the Government of Spain. Ladies and gentlemen, I want to be clear. In this situation of extraordinary crisis, ordinary legislation has proved to be inadequate and political turmoil has proved to be useless. A lot has been discussed, not only from this rostrum - and I am convinced that it will also be done by your ladies and gentlemen, especially those who fix their position on the no to the extension of the state of alarm - on the need for the state of alarm in order to maintain the exceptional situation in which we live. It has been said from some political forces, particularly conservative ones, that there were sufficient alternative tools in the existing legislation to deal with the health crisis without cutting - they say - the freedoms of citizens, and in this regard, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to share some thoughts with you. The first is that the state of alarm is fully legal, nothing less than the core of our legislative system, which is the Spanish Constitution. It is not something that has been invented, therefore, by the Spanish Government, nor has it been invented by the majority of European governments who have resorted to a kind of legal and constitutional instrument similar to ours. There are some constitutional articles that fortunately have a circumstantial use, as is the case, but which were conceived by the constituents precisely for situations in which they were precise as is the present. The second reflection that I would like to make to you is that every legal system tends to the principle of economy, which is to achieve the desired legal objectives, minimizing the resources used. Therefore, there should be no duplicate legal figures. It is therefore false to ask the Government of Spain to replace the state of alarm at this time of emergency with a rosary of ordinary laws which are designed for ordinary situations, which are not like the one we are currently living in; laws which also contain voids impossible to cover, such as the possibility of limiting mobility or also an essential basic right of our democracy, such as freedom of assembly. Such a replacement, moreover, ladies and gentlemen, would serve to hinder the legal process, divert energies that we need to resolve the health, economic and social crisis and entangle coordination between the different administrations at a critical time like the one we are experiencing. What we are going to do, ladies and gentlemen, is to plan; what we are going to do, ladies and gentlemen, is to start analysing and discussing with all of you and to negotiate the amendment of different laws to ensure proper governance, once we have raised the state of alarm. These necessary reforms are expected to be very concrete and with a single purpose, which is what I have told you before: to manage the monitoring of the pandemic once the state of alarm is lifted. These laws will be essentially health laws, i.e. the General Health Act, 1986; the Cohesion and Quality Act of the National Health System, 2003, and above all the General Public Health Act, adopted in 2011. We will also study the National Security Act, adopted in 2015, as epidemics and pandemics are among the situations of national security interest, as set out in the national security strategy. The third reflection that I would like to share with you is the following. What benefits would we gain from raising the alarm state? What benefits would we gain today from raising the alarm state? I tell you in another way, what rights are intended to be returned or restored in that way? Is it intended to restore the full right to mobility of persons without any restrictions? Do we want everyone to be able to decide whether to move from one point of our boundless geography to another? I believe that we all agree that it is not yet the time to do so and there is already a de-escalation plan which envisages the different stages in which this can be carried out gradually, in short, gradually. I would dare to say that this graduality receives the consensus of the scientists and, with few nuances - I am convinced, beyond what you are going to vote for - almost the political consensus of this House. Is it intended to restore the right to open businesses, offices, businesses without restrictions? I don't think anyone sees this as reasonable from a health point of view either. We may disagree at some speeds, at some speeds, but no one discusses the essential and central principle of gradually and safely derailing each and every economic activity in our country. Is it intended to restore the right to exercise without any limitation, without any kind of skirmish, the right of assembly, including the possibility of holding mass demonstrations or events in stadiums, concert halls or public streets? I think we will all agree that it would be an extraordinary irresponsibility, beyond what you are going to vote for. If we have all - all - come to the conclusion that some of these events can spread and spread the virus, it does not seem sensible to allow yet unlimited celebration of this kind of manifestations. What other right or freedom of citizenship has been limited by the state of alarm, ladies and gentlemen? The right to information? The right to privacy? The right to freedom of expression? The state of alarm, ladies and gentlemen, has been absolutely respectful of each and every one of the individual rights. And apart from individual rights, which are fully guaranteed, perhaps some of you believe that the ban on firing workers during the state of alarm is unfair. It is a labour measure which, indeed, the Government of Spain has adopted and which it fully assumes and which of course may not be shared by any of you, but which under no circumstances justifies the lifting of the state of alarm. The fourth and final reflection that I would like to make on this issue is that there is no contradiction between the state of alarm and the institutional descalation that returns to the full development of their competences to the autonomous communities; there is complementarity. We have already demonstrated this in the last two weeks and we will continue to demonstrate it during the coming weeks of transition to that normality; in fact, in this new extension, as I said before, the Ministry of the Interior, the Ministry of Defence and the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda will not be delegated authorities, with the Ministry of Health alone in charge, in its field, as the only exceptional command, being the health authority the reference; a command that is still necessary - I believe we all agree that the command of the Ministry of Health is still necessary - and therefore this does not exclude co-governance with those responsible for the autonomous communities. Furthermore, I would like to make a reference to this issue because this is a government - and I have said this from this rostrum not only during the adoption of the state of alarm, but also in my investiture speech - which believes in the autonomous state, which believes in the current composition of the autonomous state. The ten conferences of presidents - for the first time in the history of democracy ten conferences of presidents in ten weeks; a meeting, therefore, per week - that I have held with the presidents and autonomous presidents have been meetings of state, of joint work to attend to the Spanish citizenship, like those that I have also had with the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces and like those that I have also had with the social agents. Are those presidents' conferences any better? Without a doubt, ladies and gentlemen, this is the first time in the history of democracy that so many Presidents' conferences have been held in such a short space of time. There is no doubt that they can be improved, but they are state meetings, ladies and gentlemen, and this Government has repeated throughout the state of alarm that it would not maintain special powers any more than is necessary, no more than one day. The extension, ladies and gentlemen, which we are asking for is fifteen days. The evolution of the epidemic demands caution, the state of alarm must govern until it is necessary to partially condition certain rights for the sake of public health, and not one more day - I repeat: not one more day -. The state of alarm was dictated, extended and is intended to be extended only to save lives. Let us take this into account: only to save lives, to defend the public health of the Spaniards and, consequently, to guarantee the foundations of our own coexistence. He's never had another goal. When all this happens, which will happen, when we arrive at a new normality, or when even a future vaccine or a therapeutic remedy is implanted we access normality, dry - let us hope it is soon, ladies and gentlemen - we will have to change many things, but it will be things that we decide among all who are here, things that will decide citizenship through your political leaders, which are all of you and under no circumstances, things that have been decided or executed since and during exceptionality. I want the Spanish and you, ladies and gentlemen, to make this clear: it is a commitment from the Government to Spain. Ladies and gentlemen, I believe that all this effort we have made as a society over these ten weeks has shown that there is a future. We are getting closer and closer that Spain recovers the total, full vitality that the citizenry recovers decisively and completes its life. There is a future, therefore, and there is hope. However, this pandemic has brought us terrible economic consequences that do not affect all citizens alike. From the outset, the Government has not ceased to adopt measures with the sole objective - we have been told repeatedly - of leaving no one behind. The causes of this economic recession have absolutely nothing to do with the previous crises, in this I think we can agree, all the more reason for not applying the prescriptions that were then only used to bring in unprecedented rates of social inequality and political disaffection. If this crisis is completely different from the previous one, even more must be different, radically different, the way out of it. Of course, what we have done so far does not meet all our objectives at all, this is evident. We have to persevere, we have to stay on course, we do not have to fall into conformism or of course into self-congratulation. These actions serve to describe the way in which we have decided to move forward as a society and to express the determination with which we want to do so in the coming weeks. The estimated cost of the measures and guarantees included in the stability programme that we sent to Brussels last week is more than EUR 138.9 billion, of which EUR 106.770 million are for economic measures, EUR 4310 million have been for health measures, more than EUR 27 billion for labour measures and more than EUR 816 million for social measures. EUR 14 billion has been allocated from the Contingency Fund for the Ministry of Health for health-related expenditure, of which 98.2 % has already been allocated and EUR 889 million has been purchased in ten weeks, ladies and gentlemen. Funds worth EUR 300 million in social policy have also been distributed to the Autonomous Communities. We estimate the line of state guarantees through the ICO of EUR 100 billion for enterprises, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises and also for self-employed workers. The first three tranches worth EUR 64 500 million have been activated, 371 000 operations have been formalised and this means total financing for companies of EUR 47 751 million. These are astronomical figures. The deadline for submitting the quarterly VAT return, the split company payment and the personal income tax was extended until 20 May. The measure will benefit 95 % of businesses and also self-employed workers. It represents a liquidity contribution of more than EUR 3550 million, corresponding to EUR 11 billion of corporate tax. More than 134,442 agricultural workers included in the Special System of Self-employed Workers are being reduced by more than 19% in the resulting quotas during the inactivity periods of 2020. It was established, as you know, the impossibility of dismissing for cases related to COVID. All basic supplies for families that have become vulnerable as a result of the COVID crisis have been guaranteed. We have automatically extended unemployment benefits, more than one million self-employed workers are receiving the cessation benefit. We have allocated EUR 3 million for an extraordinary allowance for lack of activity for persons integrated into the Special System of Home Employees of the General Social Security Regime, essentially women. We have approved a retrievable paid leave from 30 March to 9 April for workers in non-essential sectors. We have set up for the autonomous communities - now in full negotiation - the Special Fund for Social Reconstruction for the amount of 16 billion euros non-refundable, for the first time in our country's history, and we have reached an agreement in defence of employment with employers and trade unions that demonstrates the height of vision - this I would also like to acknowledge from this rostrum - by the social partners in this very extraordinary situation that we are living in. The ERTEs by force majeure due to the COVID-19 may be extended, as you know, until 30 June, either by force majeure, without restarting the activity, or partial, with recovery of part of the activity, and with possibility of further extensions. Ladies and gentlemen, it is clear that the economic scenario has changed completely. Obviously, there will be a public debt leap, not only from the Spanish Government, but also from all the governments of the world. There is no other possible alternative, in this we must be absolutely clear. All States will face levels of indebtedness that were beyond their expectations in pre-pandemic situations. The Government's forecasts, which are among the most conservative, among the most prudent of all national and international agencies, foresee a decline in our economic activity of 9.2% by 2020, which can be recovered by 2021, by the time we expect growth of 6.8%. In line with the decline in activity, there is going to be a significant deterioration in our labour market, where a 9.7% drop in employment is expected by 2020. Aware of the gravity of the economic and social situation, the Government has acted, in our view, quickly and decisively. The measures taken to sustain economic activity have been successful in cushioning the destruction of employment and the disappearance of much of the productive fabric in our country. The income maintenance measures have enabled - and I think this is also an important fact that we always have to remember - to sustain 31% of the working population in our country, more than 7.2 million people who are now protected as a result of these exceptional measures. However, the economic prospects and the end of the recession are subject to a very high degree of uncertainty, as recognized by all national and international bodies, as they depend on the speed of control of the pandemic and on the absence of relapse into the descalation plan. This gigantic shock of the pandemic has reminded us all, ladies and gentlemen, how fragile we are, but its impact - and I would like to underline this from this rostrum - is not the same in society as a whole. This situation has impacted, first of all, on those people who were already vulnerable before the implosion of this pandemic in our society; they make up a collective that had not come to recover from the effects of the 2008 crisis, whose contours we are well aware of, and are also part of the coalition government programme that runs the country. Already before the appearance of COVID-19 there were 3.4 million people without income in Spain to go to a trade to replace, for example, broken clothes; 2 million compatriots could not afford an Internet connection line at home, especially in rural areas; 1.7 million people could not afford a dentist, and 1 million people could not afford a second pair of shoes. More than 2 million children in our country suffer from poverty. In this sense, this crisis resembles the classic crises. In this, it is true, the classic crises resembled; all sectors fell several steps in the pyramid of incomes: the precarious went to poverty, a part of the workers to precariousness and the middle classes became impoverished. But this crisis has a huge difference with all the previous ones: it does not hit economic sectors or companies following a logic of productivity or efficiency. The most punished sectors are not the least competitive, but the most exposed to the social restrictions resulting from the pandemic. Therefore, there are companies and there are activities that remain relatively sheltered from this new crisis, such as the production and distribution of food, while there are other activities such as, for example, the restoration - Horeca -, tourism, where the impact is clearly negative. This circumstance makes the COVID crisis, together with the traditional differences between high and low incomes, have drawn a new distinction that I would like to share with you. In the midst of the health emergency, we all naturally suffer the concern for the disease, but there are millions of people who feel relatively calm about their economic and labour future, either because they have a healthy heritage, or because they have a secure income or receive a stable salary or have a guaranteed pension. This is protected Spain; it is formed by those who have their own or external protection: the owners of income, but also employees with stable wages, insurance, such as civil servants and pensioners or public revenues. But there are many other people who don't know what's going to happen to their job because they're incurring an ERTE, or what's going to happen to their business because they've suspended their activity, or who don't just know how they're going to survive if they exhaust the unemployment benefit they've been receiving over the last few weeks. There are millions of Spaniards who wonder what is going to be in their lives. These people do not sleep calmly nor do their families. They add to the concern for their health, an even greater anguish that affects their own survival. That is the unprotected Spain, the exposed Spain, which is the one we have to embrace. They integrate that other unprotected Spain, exposed, the precarious worker, but also the self-employed; the temporary worker, but also many small and medium-sized entrepreneurs; the unemployed, the stops, but also thousands and thousands of entrepreneurs; all those who have been touched by the worst side of the health crisis, who carried out activities more exposed to social contact. Consequently, they comprise that unprotected Spain, that exposed Spain, the precarious young people, the informal ones, the unstable ones, but also traders, autonomous, professionals, entrepreneurs, a long et cetera, ladies and gentlemen. Many of the latter promoted perfectly viable professional and business projects before the start of the pandemic but are now, logically, paralysed by the health emergency and the social contact restrictions imposed. Therefore, overcoming this crisis requires a different treatment from the previous ones. Of course, no one should be left behind, unplugged. That is the commitment of the Government of Spain and in this respect we have started to act, as we have pointed out before, with subsidies, with aid and also with liquidity guarantees. Spain cannot afford the images of the queues on the doors of social dining rooms that we are seeing during these last days. Therefore, one of the main commitments of the Government of Spain is to implement minimum living income as soon as possible. It is vital, ladies and gentlemen, not to leave anyone behind, to help the most vulnerable and to prevent the thousands and thousands of civilian victims of the epidemic from being added to millions of social victims; to assist, therefore, those who need it. That will undoubtedly be the subject of the social and economic reconstruction pacts. Helping, assisting those who need it is capital, ladies and gentlemen, but it is not enough. Because in addition to assisting those who need it, we have to help those who have viable projects that have been interrupted and we also have to reintegrate workers, who can do so, as soon as possible. That should also be dealt with in the social and economic reconstruction pacts. Our response, therefore, should not only be to help and assist, but also to bet decisively on the economic revival and the return to work of millions of workers. This crisis has partially frozen our productive apparatus, but has not destroyed it. There are perfectly viable activities, there are fully healthy companies, there are promising projects that have been paralyzed. And those companies and projects cannot allow them to collapse because they also depend on millions and millions of viable, healthy and promising jobs. That is the challenge of the social and economic reconstruction pact: to help those who need it and to reactivate the economy and employment in our country. There is also broad agreement in the business and social sphere on the need to orient the activity towards a new productive model that bets on science, research, knowledge with public education at the head of it, the ecological transition, the digital transition, and also the commitment to human capital. The ecological transition is a fundamental vector of economic and social transformation, both at national and European level - that is why yesterday we approved the preliminary draft climate change law - and it is also necessary to correct the imbalances brought about since the last financial crisis in the fiscal, labour, scientific, innovation and logically also - to which it referred - of social and gender inequality in our country. Ladies and gentlemen, understanding everything we need to solve does not mean forgetting what we have achieved in the days of maximum adversity, and that is why it is essential that we continue with the unity of action, which, in my opinion, is the most effective weapon for winning the battle against the virus. In this regard, the Government is convinced that the exit from the current crisis in its triple version, not only health but also social and economic, must have three factors, in my view. The first, it is necessary to ensure that the descalation plan is implemented satisfactorily, leaving as little room as possible for outbreaks of the disease. We will not be able to reintegrate into economic activity, nor will we be able to recover economic growth and job creation if the descalation plan is urgently intensified, decoupled from scientific and expert criteria. Secondly, I believe that the Reconstruction Commission should successfully achieve a major national reconstruction agreement that brings together political forces, autonomous communities, municipalities, local authorities, social actors and also civil society as a whole. And, ladies and gentlemen, the third key element passes through Europe. I repeat, these three elements - the de-escalation plan, the national reconstruction agreement and the European financial package - are the three fundamental pillars if we are to reactivate our country's economy as soon as possible. We are facing a pandemic in which we are also fighting, I repeat, on the European front. The Spanish Government has maintained a firm position, which we have underlined and reiterated from the rostrum not only for my part, but also those responsible for negotiating it in Brussels. We have always understood that European solidarity is one of the best tools to fight the virus, but also one of the best tools to fight for the idea of Europe in which we believe, in which I believe we can be the majority of your ladies and gentlemen in agreement. Now is the time to act together, also the European front, in the face of this adversity. That is why, since the beginning of this crisis, we have been at the forefront of the negotiations, exercising leadership, building consensus in the four European Council videoconferences that have been held so far - the last on 23 April - in the numerous informal and formal meetings of the various Council formations, in which this Government has participated at all levels, and in the constant dialogue that I am holding with the President of the European Commission, with the President of the European Council and with the leaders of other Member States, especially during these last few days. The first step came when the Eurogroup announced, in early March, the easing of the public deficit criteria set out in the Stability and Growth Pact. Two weeks later, the European Central Bank launched a public debt-purchase programme valued at EUR 750 million, an unprecedented sum that will be an oxygen balloon for the Member States over the course of 2020, and the European Council on 23 April validated a triple protection network worth EUR 540 billion, in which the Spanish Government played a key role. This is something unprecedented in the history of our Union that will allow us to have unconditional liquidity lines. On the one hand, as you know, creating an unemployment reinsurance fund - which is the embryo of that unemployment fund that we want to build at European level - which will enable temporary employment regulation mechanisms to be financed by protecting workers, for an amount of EUR 100 billion at Community level. On the other hand, by financing companies through the European Investment Bank, by favouring loans at a privileged rate to guarantee liquidity, for an amount of EUR 200 billion. And, for the Member States, creating the same funding lines without conditions to deal with the health expenditure arising from COVID-19, for an amount of EUR 240 billion. In total, I insist, EUR 540 billion. At that European Council, the Government of Spain made a commitment that this financial protection network should be put in place from 1 June, we have achieved that, and with that we have given a first message of confidence and security to our compatriots, to our companies, aware that this was the first step, that of the short term, on the road to the economic and social resolution of the crisis. We also welcomed the road map for a coordinated de-escalation by the European Commission, we called for the same coordination for the restoration of internal movements within the European Union, and also on Monday we received positively the joint initiative of the French and German governments for the European recovery of COVID-19, which contains, as you know, many of the proposals made by the Spanish Government for a rapid and solidarity-based exit from the crisis. The European recovery fund proposed by these two governments is at least EUR 500 billion in transfers to countries and sectors affected by the crisis, which I believe is a good step in the direction that our country needs and that the European Union as a whole needs. The Government now hopes that the European Commission will put on the table in the coming days a proposal, even more ambitious, which will serve as the basis for an agreement on which the economic and social revival of our continent will be affected, as - I repeat - was entrusted by the European Council on 23 April. Now, ladies and gentlemen, it is time to look further, to think about the medium and long term and the social and economic recovery of the continent through the articulation of a great agreement between the Member States of the Union. Ladies and gentlemen, the victory on the health front is getting closer every day, there is the data; there is a future thanks to the sacrifice of all. We have come this far thanks to the exceptional effort of workers on the health front, pharmacists, transporters, supermarket employees, cleaning staff, merchants, State Security Forces and Corpses, the army, firefighters, vigilantes and thousands of volunteers; in short, thanks to the solidarity of the whole of Spanish society and its enormous sense of responsibility at this critical and hard time that our country is suffering. We warmly thank all of them and all of them, on behalf of the Government of Spain. [[Applauds]] Unity, ladies and gentlemen, is the most powerful force; it is the most powerful force. Adversities are superimposed not on the groups with stronger individuals, but on the more united groups. Unity has saved thousands and thousands of lives in our country, and now we need that unity to extend also to the economic, social and political front to combat the economic and social crisis, because unity can allow us to save many lives, many businesses and also jobs. The world is experiencing the passage of a pandemic, the worst in a hundred years, and we continue to fight it on all fronts, that is the simple summary of the last weeks of our lives. A deadly pandemic; a global pandemic that has paralysed our country and the whole world; an unknown pandemic, fast, fierce, changing, taking lives, destroying businesses, sowing desolation and social helplessness. And we're beating her, ladies and gentlemen, on the health front. Now we are immersed in an economic and social crisis of frightening proportions as a result of the COVID, but we are not going to get carried away by that fear. We can overcome it, and I am convinced that we will overcome it from unity. Today I would like to end my speech from this rostrum, ladies and gentlemen, with a recognition of all the public representatives who have been responsible for living these terrible weeks from a post of command, from the arena of direct management. I thank all the representatives of all the political parties present in this House, in all the territories, in all the agencies, institutions and scales of the Administration. It is a sincere recognition that I want to make from gratitude, companionship at such critical times and deep respect. To all of them, to all of them, I would like to transfer my understanding to the mistakes that they have been able to make, given the complexity and drama of the decisions they must take, because of the urgency of the measures they had to take and because of the difficulties in finding the necessary material resources in time. And, in the same way, I would like to apologize to the citizens for their own mistakes, which have always been dictated by urgency in times, by the scarcity of resources, by the exceptionality and absence of precedents of the crisis and by their unusual size. Because, ladies and gentlemen, beyond the specific differences of opinion, beyond the hasty statements or the background noise that we have heard on many occasions, we have had to work side by side; all of us have been united in the same desire: to save lives and to serve our country in the midst of the most adverse situation we could imagine. Today, and so I would like it to appear in the Journal of Sessions, I want to publicly acknowledge their management, that of each and every one of them, and I want to do so with the same emphasis with which I support the management of my Government during these critical weeks. There is no possible satisfaction in the face of a crisis involving the loss of human beings; any balance will fill us with grief. Spain, as I have already said, will declare official mourning at the moment when the whole territory enters phase 1 of the descalade. And as soon as we find ourselves in this new normality, with all the guarantees of health security we will celebrate the well-deserved public tribute to the victims of COVID. Ladies and gentlemen, we have been subjected to an unimaginable test, we have responded with sacrifice, with unity, with a morale of victory, and we are overcoming this test. Let us recognize all the good we have done together and how brave we have been along the unusual path of these past ten weeks. We must assess the agreements that we have been able to reach and their beneficial consequences for the whole of Spanish citizenship, far above the differences or mistakes that made their scope difficult. Because it will be in that recognition where we will find the strength necessary to rebuild, reactivate and advance our country, now that we can prudently calculate a provisional end to the exceptional time that we have had to live for ten weeks in this part. Ladies and gentlemen, we have stopped the virus together, we must now end this victory with unity, and we must undertake, together too - and that is what I appeal to - the social and economic reconstruction of our country. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

University scholarships are fine, but lower tuition fees so people can study. Bank loans to pay the rent replace the one that does not suspend the rents. The third thing you do is deploy control measures and state security forces and bodies throughout the territory. You have put more than a million fines, by neighborhoods - it has already been said several times in this rostrum -. You have been tasked with sending a clear message: self-determination will not be in dialogue nor will it be questionable nor will it enter. ParlaMint-ES_2020-05-20-CD200520.u63	Thank you, President. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen, for your words and also for your contribution to this debate. But I want you to know that beyond the fact that I can empathize with the recurring aspects of this debate, beyond the fact that I can also empathize with the desire that we all have - and that I think it represents the whole of Spanish society - to recover our normality as soon as possible, and therefore this Congress recovers and resumes the parliamentary activity that is most typical of our daily lives, before the pandemic emerges with its raw reality, I obviously believe that we have many things to fulfil in this vote. In this vote, ladies and gentlemen, we are going to empathize with a majority of Spanish society. The majority feeling of Spanish society is that we are winning the battle against the virus; we are winning it. We are getting closer and closer to finally ending the tragic social, economic and institutional consequences of the state of alarm, getting closer and closer to winning, at least in this first wave, the virus and getting together with the best instruments, with the best tools to be able to cope with it in the second wave or in the next bursts, which will no doubt come. I used to comment with the first Vice-President that it is indeed the fifth time that we have come to this House to ask for the approval of the state of alarm and, then, its extensions, and I believe that it is also a healthy exercise of democracy that demonstrates, moreover, the clear will of the Government of Spain and the commitment to this House and to accountability and to be absolutely transparent in the exercise and implementation of the state of alarm. If you look outside our country, you will see exactly what the behaviour of other European governments is and what decisions they have made when they have requested a state of alarm. I do not believe that there has been any government in Europe that has submitted every fifteen days to the approval of an extension of the state of alarm. I insist that there are countries that have requested the state of alarm for six months, and even more. I therefore believe that, from the Government's point of view, we should at least recognize that exercise of transparency and accountability, which I would also like to extend to other issues. It is said here by those parliamentary groups who do not want to support the extension of the state of alarm that this is a government that does not answer, that is not transparent, that does not report. It is important to give some figures so that they can at least be included in the minutes of this debate in the Journal of Sessions. I know you are not going to convince those who have previously decided to vote against the state of alarm and its extension, but from 13 January to 19 May we will give some examples of what the Government has done. The government as a whole has held a total of 149 press briefings, with 1350 questions answered, 255 to me as President of the Government, including the daily lives of the health technicians, those of the Technical Management Committee, mine as President, those of the Minister of Health, together with the director of the CCAES, Dr. Simon, with practically all the ministries of our Government. An average of almost 20 questions per press conference, ladies and gentlemen: in which there were 11 fewer questions and in which there were 34 more questions. Maximum information transparency. Let us ask what the presidents and autonomous presidents are doing to see if there is a greater degree of accountability, both in Parliament and in the media, about the management that can be done in relation to this crisis. There have been 119 meetings of the Evaluation and Monitoring Committee, 52 meetings of the Technical Management Committee since 17 March, including the 7 meetings of the Descaled Committee. The deployment of coordination and accountability, ladies and gentlemen, is not there. There have been 82 interterritorial and sectoral meetings between the Government of Spain, the various departments and the councils referred to. There have been 10 conferences of autonomous presidents. As I said before, there have never been so many conferences of presidents in the history of democracy. In the last sixteen years, ladies and gentlemen, how many conferences of presidents have been held in our country? It's been six. There's been 10 in 10 weeks. There have been 30 meetings of the Inter-Territorial Health Council, not counting the bilateral phase-by-step meetings. Multitudes of sectoral conferences of inter-territorial councils on all areas of the Executive, 124 international meetings, 8 meetings of the Scientific Committee, 28 meetings with social partners, 78 meetings with other representatives and institutions. This is a government, ladies and gentlemen, which has tried to exercise dialogue, negotiation and agreement with each and every area of the composite State in which we are and also with the social partners. And at European level too. Since February alone, ladies and gentlemen, there have been a total of 23 councils of ministers, and in Congress there have been 9 appearances, both with my presence as President and with that of various ministers, with various agendas of the day. In addition, 23 ministerial appearances have been made before the Committees of the Congress of Deputies. Since the beginning of this crisis and the state of alarm, the Minister of Health has appeared in the Parliamentary Committee on Health to report and transfer daily all information on the evolution of the pandemic to his lords. There have been three control sessions in the Senate with various ministers and, indeed, a control session with me as President of the Government. In ordinary times there has never been this accountability, ladies and gentlemen! And, in extraordinary times, it is not that we are accounting, it is that we are daily sharing with all of you, with all the parliamentary groups and with the social actors the decisions we are making. Therefore, transparency, coordination, accountability. And I am convinced - and I said this at the beginning of my first speech - that we can certainly improve, that there are aspects of improvement in all governments in our environment and also in ours. That 1981 Act regulates - as we have discussed in some other extensions - the state of alarm, emergency and site, and that Act, in its chapter 2, deals precisely with the state of alarm and explains in which situations the state of alarm should be declared. I would like to record in the Journal of Sessions some of the issues referred to in this organic law. For example, Article 4 reads as follows: 'The Government, in use of the powers conferred on it by Article 116.2 of the Spanish Constitution, may declare the state of alarm, in whole or in part of the national territory, when there are any of the following serious disturbances of normalcy: (a) Disasters, calamities or public misfortunes, such as earthquakes, floods, urban and forest fires or major accidents. (b) health crises, such as epidemics and serious pollution.' And it then mentions, ladies and gentlemen, other situations of paralysis of essential public services for the community or of destitution of basic necessities. Therefore, Mr Casado, the law envisages the state of alarm precisely for cases of health crises such as epidemics, such as the one we are living in right now in our country. And I ask you, Your Honor, because you are the main leader of the opposition in our country: Do you know that this is the most serious epidemic that has occurred in Spain in the last century? That this is the most serious epidemic since that law was passed in 1981? If not now, Your Honor, in what situation of health crisis or epidemic do you consider that the state of alarm should be applied in our country? [[Applauds]] Do you find a few 27,000 dead in our country, Your Honor? Do you think 2 million people are infected, according to the seroprevalence study? Do you find little the situation that our hospitals, our healthcare professionals, had to live on the brink of collapse, especially in those autonomous communities that you govern? What would have to happen, ladies and gentlemen, if you were to consider that there is a serious health crisis or an epidemic in order to be able to approve the state of alarm? And so it has been. Thanks to the extraordinary effort of teachers and students during this period, the course will be completed on time and with academic rigour. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-05-20-CD200520.u223	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, Minister, they say that those sentenced to death really start dying the night before the execution. That is the feeling that we have some of the Members who are here today, those who represent the communities that are going to suffer the worst in the biggest economic crisis in this country since the post-war period. Spain and the whole Union are going to pay this year for the consequences of the confinement and paralysis of the productive sector. [[The Lord]] Experts warn us that we are going to have a crisis in uve, a sharp fall over this year around 9 points of GDP and a recovery that will begin next year. But there are other areas, Minister, within this country where the effects of the pandemic crisis will be lethal. Ladies and gentlemen, there is a huge difference between a crisis and the failure of an economic model. The Canary Islands, as is largely the case in the Balearic Islands, have lost their way of life overnight. In Europe they are concerned about the damage that will result from the loss of tourism in countries such as Greece where it accounts for 24% of its GDP, or in Italy or Spain where it accounts for 13%. In our country we are alarmed by the damage of an activity that employs 12% of workers, but Spain has a powerful export sector based on industry and agriculture. Can you imagine what kind of disaster it is for a territory to lose between 30 and 40% of its GDP and lose 50% of its employment overnight? For these are the figures that are stalling in the Canary Islands, ladies and gentlemen, just those figures. In the Canary Islands, tourism is the driving force behind agriculture and local industry, which live on the inputs of the tourism sector. In a few weeks we have lost a market of 16 million high-level buyers who came every year to the Canary Islands and who each day assumed a floating population of about 300,000 people who rented cars, ate in restaurants, took taxis, visited museums and bought in our shops. All this, Minister, has disappeared, practically overnight without giving us time to prepare. The Canary Islands will lose 12 billion sales of tourism services this year. Right now we have more than 480,000 workers out of the labour market and by the end of the year our unemployment can exceed 50% of the active population of the islands. What is happening now in the Canary Islands is not an economic crisis but a social catastrophe. It is the absolute failure of an economic model that will cause the extinction of the welfare state we enjoyed in our land. No society can survive when it loses half its workforce and the economic sector that creates more jobs and wealth. Madam Minister, I come to this Congress to ask exactly what Spain is asking of the European Union: understanding and solidarity. All the countries of the Union will need exceptional measures to be able to overcome the economic turbulence caused by the health crisis. There is the MEDE, the SURE, the EIB, and the European Central Bank are ready to support each country's debt emissions. We are in extraordinary circumstances that require extraordinary answers. For the tourist archipelagos of Spain the loss of tourism is not an adversity, it is a tragedy. For the Canary Islands to lose 12 million tourists this year is equivalent to an unprecedented catastrophe, as if a volcano broke out in the middle of the islands. The consequences of this impact will cause the most extreme misery and despair in the vulnerable families of our islands. Spain already has data on the instruments available from the European Union. Madam Minister, the Spanish Autonomous Communities have no idea what tools we are going to have. It is time for the Government of Spain to take a step forward. Within our country's recovery plans, it is essential to establish a rescue plan for those territories where the consequences of the social changes caused by the coronavirus compromise the survival of the welfare state. In the case of the Canary Islands, ladies and gentlemen, an extraordinary line of State funding is needed to cover social expenditure and an investment plan capable of keeping our economy alive. The Government of Spain has made an intelligent decision by taking the extension of force majeure ERTs to allow, to the extent possible, the rescue of jobs as soon as the economy of our country returns to functioning. But in the case of communities like the Canary Islands, we are talking about a structural crisis that is going to prolong, hopefully, at least a year and a half. The rescue plan for the Canary Islands must therefore be considered within the multiannual framework of two budgets until we determine to what extent the recovery of a market that has disappeared today due to the insecurity created by the pandemic takes place. That is why we have reached the agreement to extend the ERTEs of the Canary Islands tourism sector until the international markets are open. As you will know, the high season of tourism is winter and, if it is necessary to extend the ERTE until December, it will have to do so, Your Honor, because it is about maintaining the canary productive fabric and the jobs, which are not fixed discontinuous as in other communities in which the season ends in September. In short, Madam Minister, as I said before, in the face of extraordinary circumstances, extraordinary measures are needed. And it's just like that. If we want to save society, we need extraordinary funding, and we also need the Government of Spain to allow our community to go to the debt market to also finance us through our own future efforts. As in that film, The Return to the World in Eighty Days, if we want to get alive at the end of this crisis, we need to burn even the furniture so that the ship will stay on course. It is time to use the surplus and the treasury remnants of local corporations, the time to borrow as much as we can pay in the coming years. It is time to save the welfare state that we have built with great effort in these forty years of democracy. Ladies and gentlemen, the Canary Islands needs active employment policies that have taken away from us, that are able to keep in operation and in the provision of services of public interest a significant part of that half a million workers who are going to be expelled from the labour market. And we need to push forward the construction with an extraordinary plan of investments in public works that allows the survival of a sector that is right now the only alternative to cushion the destruction caused by the loss of the tourist market.
